<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

## user

&#x20;I want to make sure that I can... I want to make sure that the ability to turn off someone's ability to access... I want to be able to CLIck on a student, see the last time they were on. I want to see everything they did with, its questions, drills, whatever. And I also want to have it so when I CLIck in the teacher skill of HAPI teacher's workspace and I CLIck their name, it should be fixed. We should be able to toggle it off, and then they lose access. I also want to add on my thing, I want to give a timer. It's not a timer, but I want to actually easily set the amount. So if you have a number, like just a number beside each person's place where I can talk to them, I could put a number, five, three, double amount of days, or like 10 days I can choose. You can /grill-me if you have any questions.


## assistant

First question: when a teacher turns a student’s access off, should the student immediately lose access to all LSAT U content while their account, history, assignments, and messages remain intact?

My recommendation: yes. Make it a reversible suspension, not deletion. Turning access back on restores everything exactly as it was.

## user

&#x20;Yes. Yes, but their account history, assignments, and messages should remain intact? Yes, it should. They should lose access immediately. And it doesn't need to email them as it does right now.


## assistant

When a suspended student tries to log in, what should they see?

My recommendation: allow login, but replace the normal app with a simple “Your access is currently inactive. Contact your instructor.” screen. They should still be able to see and send messages so they can contact you, but they should not access lessons, drills, questions, analytics, or assignments.

You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Human approves the diff before any commit. Use when the user says \\"/codex-build\\", \\"have codex build this\\", \\"codex implement the plan\\", \\"hand the plan to codex\\", \\"delegate the build to codex\\", or right after a plan survives /grill-me-codex, /grill-with-docs-codex, or /codex-review and they choose Codex for implementation (Act 3). Also for standalone delegation: refactors, mechanical migrations, bug fixes with a known repro, test/coverage writing — anything that reads as a work order. NOT for tiny edits (~<20 lines — delegation overhead loses), NOT for design work (if writing the spec forces decisions, that\'s /grill-me-codex first), NOT for reviewing existing code (/codex:review), and NOT for anything needing Claude-session tools (MCP, secrets, browser).\r\nskills\\INDEX.md:98:- **domain-modeling** — Build and sharpen a project\'s domain model. ACT 1 (you ↔ Claude) — Claude interviews you relentlessly about a plan or design, one question at a time, recommending an answer for each and exploring the codebase when it can answer itself, until every branch of the decision tree is resolved. ACT 2 (Claude ↔ Codex) — Claude writes the locked plan to PLAN.md and OpenAI Codex adversarially reviews it in a read-only sandbox (VERDICT:APPROVED/REVISE), Claude revises and re-submits to the SAME Codex session until APPROVED or a MAX_ROUNDS cap, then you sign off before any code. Use when the user says "/grill-me-codex", "grill me then have codex review", "grill me and stress-test the plan", "interview me about this plan then get a second model on it", or is about to build something high-stakes (auth, schema, concurrency, migrations, payments) and wants both alignment AND a cross-model sanity check before implementation. Builds on Matt Pocock\'s grill-me (MIT). For the docs-aware variant use /grill-with-docs-codex; if you already have a plan and want only the Codex review use /codex-review. NOT for reviewing already-written code (use /codex:review) and NOT for trivial changes.\r\nskills\\INDEX.md:112:- **grill-with-docs** — A relentless interview to sharpen a plan or design, which also creates docs (ADR\'s and glossary) as we go.\r\nskills\\INDEX.md:113:- **grill-with-docs-codex** — Two-act plan hardening with living documentation. ACT 1 (you ↔ Claude) — Claude interviews you relentlessly about a plan, one question at a time, challenging it against your project\'s existing domain model and glossary (CONTEXT.md), sharpening fuzzy terms, stress-testing with concrete scenarios, cross-referencing code, and updating CONTEXT.md + ADRs inline as decisions crystallise. ACT 2 (Claude ↔ Codex) — Claude writes the locked plan to PLAN.md and OpenAI Codex adversarially reviews it in a read-only sandbox (VERDICT:APPROVED/REVISE), Claude revises and re-submits to the SAME Codex session until APPROVED or a MAX_ROUNDS cap, then you sign off before any code. Use when the user says "/grill-with-docs-codex", "grill me against the docs then have codex review", "stress-test this against our domain model then get a second model on it", or is about to build something high-stakes in a project with established terminology/ADRs and wants alignment, documentation, AND a cross-model sanity check. Builds on Matt Pocock\'s grill-with-docs (MIT). NOT for reviewing already-written code (use /codex:review) and NOT for trivial changes.\r\nskills\\INDEX.md:114:- **grilling** — Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Newer builds do evaluate them, but they are spilling array functions and an openpyxl-written file has no spill metadata, so only the top-left cell of the range gets a value — and `recalc.py` reports `total_errors: 0` on the truncated result. Use `INDEX`/`MATCH` for lookups, and sort, filter, and de-duplicate in Python before writing the cells.\nskills\\claude\\_inactive\\yt\\SKILL.md:6:compatibility: Requires internet access to reach transcriptapi.com. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: setup-matt-pocock-skills\ndescription: "Configure this repo for the engineering skills: set up its issue tracker, triage label vocabulary, and domain doc layout. Run once before first use of the other engineering skills."\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Setup Matt Pocock\'s Skills\n\nScaffold the per-repo configuration that the engineering skills assume:\n\n- **Issue tracker**: where issues live (GitHub by default; local markdown is also supported out of the box)\n- **Triage labels**: the strings used for the five canonical triage roles\n- **Domain docs**: where `CONTEXT.md` and ADRs live, and the consumer rules for reading them\n\nThis is a prompt-driven skill, not a deterministic script. Explore, present what you found, confirm with the user, then write.\n\n## Process\n\n### 1. Explore\n\nLook at the current repo to understand its starting state. Read whatever exists; don\'t assume:\n\n- `git remote -v` and `.git/config`: is this a GitHub repo? Which one?\n- `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` at the repo root: does either exist? Is there already an `## Agent skills` section in either?\n- `CONTEXT.md` and `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the repo root\n- `docs/adr/` and any `src/*/docs/adr/` directories\n- `docs/agents/`: does this skill\'s prior output already exist?\n- `.scratch/`: a sign that a local-markdown issue tracker convention is already in use\n- Is the `triage` skill installed? (a `triage` skill folder alongside this one, or `triage` in your available skills.) This decides whether Section B runs at all.\n- Monorepo signals: a `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, a `workspaces` field in `package.json`, or a populated `packages/*` with its own `src/`. These are present only in a genuinely large multi-package repo; their absence means single-context, which is almost every repo.\n\n### 2. Present findings and ask\n\nSummarise what\'s present and what\'s missing. Then take the sections in order. One section, one answer, then the next.\n\nLead each section with the recommended answer so the user can accept it in a word. Give a one-line explainer only when the choice genuinely branches; skip the section entirely when exploration already settled it (Section B when `triage` isn\'t installed, Section C when there\'s no monorepo).\n\n**Section A: Issue tracker.**\n\n> Explainer: The "issue tracker" is where issues live for this repo. Skills like `to-tickets`, `triage`, and `to-spec` read from and write to it. They need to know whether to call `gh issue create`, write a markdown file under `.scratch/`, or follow some other workflow you describe. Pick the place you actually track work for this repo.\n\nDefault posture: these skills were designed for GitHub. If a `git remote` points at GitHub, propose that. If a `git remote` points at GitLab (`gitlab.com` or a self-hosted host), propose GitLab. Otherwise (or if the user prefers), offer:\n\n- **GitHub**: issues live in the repo\'s GitHub Issues (uses the `gh` CLI)\n- **GitLab**: issues live in the repo\'s GitLab Issues (uses the [`glab`](https://gitlab.com/gitlab-org/cli) CLI)\n- **Local markdown**: issues live as files under `.scratch/<feature>/` in this repo (good for solo projects or repos without a remote)\n- **Other** (Jira, Linear, etc.): ask the user to describe the workflow in one paragraph; the skill will record it as freeform prose\n\nRecord the choice in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`. The GitHub and GitLab templates carry a "PRs as a request surface" flag, defaulted **off**. Leave it off and don\'t raise it: a user who wants external PRs in the triage queue can flip the flag in the file later.\n\n**Section B: Triage label vocabulary.** Skip this section entirely if the `triage` skill isn\'t installed (exploration told you), since an uninstalled skill needs no labels.\n\nIf it is installed, ask exactly one question:\n\n> Do you want to keep the default triage labels? (recommended: **yes**)\n\nThe defaults are the five canonical roles, each label string equal to its name: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. On **yes**, write them as-is. Only if the user says no, usually because their tracker already uses other names (e.g. `bug:triage` for `needs-triage`), collect the overrides so `triage` applies existing labels instead of creating duplicates.\n\n**Section C: Domain docs.** Default to **single-context** (one `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the repo root). This fits almost every repo; write it without asking.\n\nOffer **multi-context** (a root `CONTEXT-MAP.md` pointing to per-context `CONTEXT.md` files) only when exploration found monorepo signals. Then confirm which layout they want.\n\n### 3. Confirm and edit\n\nShow the user a draft of:\n\n- The `## Agent skills` block to add to whichever of `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` is being edited (see step 4 for selection rules)\n- The contents of `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, `docs/agents/domain.md`, and `docs/agents/triage-labels.md` (the last only when `triage` is installed)\n\nLet them edit before writing.\n\n### 4. Write\n\n**Pick the file to edit:**\n\n- If `CLAUDE.md` exists, edit it.\n- Else if `AGENTS.md` exists, edit it.\n- If neither exists, ask the user which one to create; don\'t pick for them.\n\nNever create `AGENTS.md` when `CLAUDE.md` already exists (or vice versa); always edit the one that\'s already there.\n\nIf an `## Agent skills` block already exists in the chosen file, update its contents in-place rather than appending a duplicate. Don\'t overwrite user edits to the surrounding sections.\n\nThe block:\n\n```markdown\n## Agent skills\n\n### Issue tracker\n\n[one-line summary of where issues are tracked]. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\n\n### Triage labels\n\n[one-line summary of the label vocabulary]. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\n\n### Domain docs\n\n[one-line summary of layout: "single-context" or "multi-context"]. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\n```\n\nInclude the `### Triage labels` sub-block, and write `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`, only when `triage` is installed and Section B ran. When it isn\'t, both are omitted.\n\nThen write the docs files using the seed templates in this skill folder as a starting point:\n\n- [issue-tracker-github.md](./issue-tracker-github.md): GitHub issue tracker\n- [issue-tracker-gitlab.md](./issue-tracker-gitlab.md): GitLab issue tracker\n- [issue-tracker-local.md](./issue-tracker-local.md): local-markdown issue tracker\n- [triage-labels.md](./triage-labels.md): label mapping (only if `triage` is installed)\n- [domain.md](./domain.md): domain doc consumer rules + layout\n\nFor "other" issue trackers, write `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` from scratch using the user\'s description.\n\n### 5. Done\n\nTell the user the setup is complete and which engineering skills will now read from these files. Mention they can edit `docs/agents/*.md` directly later; re-running this skill is only necessary if they want to switch issue trackers or restart from scratch.\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: grill-with-docs\ndescription: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan or design, which also creates docs (ADR\'s and glossary) as we go.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\nCall the Skill tool twice, for "grilling" and "domain-modeling".\n\r\n---\nname: grilling\ndescription: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any \'grill\' trigger phrases.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\nInterview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\n\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven\'t heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user\'s answers before the next round.\n\nFormat a round like so:\n\n```\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n\n---\n\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\n\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\n```\n\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.\n\nFinding _facts_ is your job, never the user\'s. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don\'t ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don\'t block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user\'s: put each to them and wait.\n\nThe session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.\n\r\n---\nname: domain-modeling\ndescription: Build and sharpen a project\'s domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\n---\n\n# Domain Modeling\n\nActively build and sharpen the project\'s domain model as you design. This is the *active* discipline: challenging terms, inventing edge-case scenarios, and writing the glossary and decisions down the moment they crystallise. (Merely *reading* `CONTEXT.md` for vocabulary is not this skill: that\'s a one-line habit any skill can do. This skill is for when you\'re changing the model, not just consuming it.)\n\n## File structure\n\nMost repos have a single context:\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT.md\n├── docs/\n│   └── adr/\n│       ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n│       └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n└── src/\n```\n\nIf a `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists at the root, the repo has multiple contexts. The map points to where each one lives:\n\n```\n/\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\n├── docs/\n│   └── adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\n├── src/\n│   ├── ordering/\n│   │   ├── CONTEXT.md\n│   │   └── docs/adr/                 ← context-specific decisions\n│   └── billing/\n│       ├── CONTEXT.md\n│       └── docs/adr/\n```\n\nCreate files lazily: only when you have something to write. If no `CONTEXT.md` exists, create one when the first term is resolved. If no `docs/adr/` exists, create it when the first ADR is needed.\n\n## During the session\n\n### Challenge against the glossary\n\nWhen the user uses a term that conflicts with the existing language in `CONTEXT.md`, call it out immediately. "Your glossary defines \'cancellation\' as X, but you seem to mean Y. Which is it?"\n\n### Sharpen fuzzy language\n\nWhen the user uses vague or overloaded terms, propose a precise canonical term. "You\'re saying \'account\': do you mean the Customer or the User? Those are different things."\n\n### Discuss concrete scenarios\n\nWhen domain relationships are being discussed, stress-test them with specific scenarios. Invent scenarios that probe edge cases and force the user to be precise about the boundaries between concepts.\n\n### Cross-reference with code\n\nWhen the user states how something works, check whether the code agrees. If you find a contradiction, surface it: "Your code cancels entire Orders, but you just said partial cancellation is possible. Which is right?"\n\n### Update CONTEXT.md inline\n\nWhen a term is resolved, update `CONTEXT.md` right there. Don\'t batch these up: capture them as they happen. Use the format in [CONTEXT-FORMAT.md](./CONTEXT-FORMAT.md).\n\n`CONTEXT.md` should be totally devoid of implementation details. Do not treat `CONTEXT.md` as a spec, a scratch pad, or a repository for implementation decisions. It is a glossary and nothing else.\n\n### Offer ADRs sparingly\n\nOnly offer to create an ADR when all three are true:\n\n1. **Hard to reverse**: the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful\n2. **Surprising without context**: a future reader will wonder "why did they do it this way?"\n3. **The result of a real trade-off**: there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons\n\nIf any of the three is missing, skip the ADR. Use the format in [ADR-FORMAT.md](./ADR-FORMAT.md).\n\r\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.9 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '#!/bin/sh\n# Validate the skills DB model (claude/ + codex/ subtrees, activations, INDEX).\n# Logic lives in check-skills.py; this wrapper keeps the historic entrypoint.\n# POSIX sh on purpose: must behave identically under `sh`, `bash`, and `zsh`.\nset -eu\nSCRIPT_DIR=$(CDPATH= cd -- "$(dirname -- "$0")" && pwd -P)\nROOT=$(CDPATH= cd -- "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd -P)\nexec /usr/bin/env python3 "$ROOT/system/scripts/check-skills.py" "$@"\n\r\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n"""Validate the skills DB model (replaces the old \'expect exactly 11\' check).\n\nActivation model (changed 2026-07-17, Joshua\'s call): a project activates a DB skill\nby COPYING it into `.claude/skills/<name>/` (or `.agents/skills/`), not symlinking.\nCopies are real files — portable, committable, no dead links on another machine.\nThe tradeoff: a copy does not auto-follow DB edits. This script\'s (e) check exists\nbecause of that tradeoff — it flags copies that have drifted from their DB source.\nSymlinks are still tolerated (not an error) for any that predate the change or for\nthe unrelated vendor-plugin mechanism in the DB itself (skills/claude/_snapshots\npattern), which this script does not touch.\n\nChecks:\n  (a) every activation in an active root resolves to real content (no broken symlink);\n  (b) no skill lives in an active root un-mirrored (real dir with no DB counterpart);\n  (c) INDEX.md covers every top-level DB skill;\n  (d) no duplicate canonical name within a subtree;\n  (e) a copied (non-symlink) activation\'s SKILL.md matches its DB source byte-for-byte\n      — catches silent drift after the DB is edited but the activation isn\'t re-synced.\n  (f) every multi-segment path cited in CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md resolves on disk\n      — routing maps that point at moved or deleted files send sessions hunting.\n  (g) no UNDECLARED claude/codex asymmetry. The two subtrees are intentionally unequal,\n      so the legitimate split is declared in skills/PARITY.md and only new drift fails.\n  (h) effective Claude/Codex skill availability has no undeclared one-runtime drift;\n  (i) active instruction scopes have both CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md;\n  (j) Codex agent TOML files are valid, complete, and activated from the catalog.\nExit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL.\n"""\nimport os, re, sys, filecmp, tomllib\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nBRAIN = str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2])\nDB = os.path.join(BRAIN, "skills")\nHOME = str(Path.home())\nSPECIAL = {"_snapshots", "_project-forks", "_inactive", "README.md", "INDEX.md"}\n\n# Active roots to audit (only those that exist are checked).\nPROJECT_ACTIVE_ROOTS = [\n    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills"),\n    os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills"),\n    os.path.join(BRAIN, "projects", "email-hq", ".claude", "skills"),\n]\n\n# Home-level roots also contain skills installed and managed by external plugins.\n# They are included when comparing effective Claude/Codex availability, but they\n# are not required to be mirrored into this project\'s catalog.\nRUNTIME_ACTIVE_ROOTS = {\n    "claude": [os.path.join(HOME, ".claude", "skills"),\n               os.path.join(BRAIN, ".claude", "skills")],\n    "codex": [os.path.join(HOME, ".agents", "skills"),\n              os.path.join(BRAIN, ".agents", "skills")],\n}\n\ndef db_names(sub):\n    base = os.path.join(DB, sub)\n    if not os.path.isdir(base):\n        return set()\n    return {n for n in os.listdir(base)\n            if n not in SPECIAL and (os.path.isdir(os.path.join(base, n)) or os.path.islink(os.path.join(base, n)))}\n\ndef main():\n    fails = []\n    # DB structure\n    for sub in ("claude", "codex"):\n        if not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(DB, sub)):\n            fails.append(f"DB subtree missing: skills/{sub}")\n    claude_db = db_names("claude")\n    codex_db = db_names("codex")\n\n    # (d) duplicate canonical names within a subtree — filesystem guarantees uniqueness,\n    # but validate SKILL.md name matches dir name where a SKILL.md exists.\n    for sub, names in (("claude", claude_db), ("codex", codex_db)):\n        for n in names:\n            # Vendor entries are symlinks whose dir name may be disambiguated\n            # (e.g. discord-access -> plugin skill named \'access\'); skip name check.\n            if os.path.islink(os.path.join(DB, sub, n)):\n                continue\n            real = os.path.realpath(os.path.join(DB, sub, n))\n            skf = os.path.join(real, "SKILL.md")\n            if os.path.isfile(skf):\n                with open(skf, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:\n                    head = f.read(1200)\n                m = re.search(r"(?mi)^name:\\s*(.+)$", head)\n                if m and m.group(1).strip().strip(\'"\').strip("\'") != n:\n                    fails.append(f"name mismatch: skills/{sub}/{n} declares \'{m.group(1).strip()}\'")\n\n    # (a)+(b) active roots\n    all_db = claude_db | codex_db\n    for root in PROJECT_ACTIVE_ROOTS:\n        if not os.path.isdir(root):\n            continue\n        for n in os.listdir(root):\n            if n.startswith("."):\n                continue\n            p = os.path.join(root, n)\n            if os.path.islink(p):\n                if not os.path.exists(p):\n                    fails.append(f"broken activation symlink: {root}/{n}")\n                elif n not in all_db:\n                    fails.append(f"activation not represented in DB: {root}/{n} -> {os.path.realpath(p)}")\n            elif os.path.isdir(p):\n                # real dir in an active root: must be mirrored in DB by name\n                if n not in all_db:\n                    fails.append(f"un-mirrored skill in active root: {root}/{n} (not in DB)")\n                    continue\n                # (e) drift check: copied activation\'s SKILL.md vs its DB source\n                sub = "codex" if ".agents" in Path(root).parts else "claude"\n                db_dir = os.path.join(DB, sub, n)\n                act_skf = os.path.join(p, "SKILL.md")\n                db_skf = os.path.join(db_dir, "SKILL.md")\n                if os.path.isfile(act_skf) and os.path.isfile(db_skf):\n                    if not filecmp.cmp(act_skf, db_skf, shallow=False):\n                        fails.append(f"stale activation copy: {root}/{n} differs from skills/{sub}/{n} — re-copy from DB")\n\n    # (c) INDEX coverage\n    idx = os.path.join(DB, "INDEX.md")\n    if not os.path.isfile(idx):\n        fails.append("INDEX.md missing")\n    else:\n        with open(idx, encoding="utf-8") as f:\n            itext = f.read()\n        indexed = set(re.findall(r"^- \\*\\*(?:[a-z0-9-]+: )?([^*]+)\\*\\*", itext, re.M))\n        for sub, names in (("claude", claude_db), ("codex", codex_db)):\n            for n in names:\n                if n not in indexed:\n                    fails.append(f"INDEX missing DB skill: {sub}/{n}")\n\n    # (f) dead paths in the routing maps. Only multi-segment paths (a/b) are checked;\n    # bare fragments like `codex/` are prose, not routing targets.\n    for fname in ("CLAUDE.md", "AGENTS.md"):\n        fpath = os.path.join(BRAIN, fname)\n        if not os.path.isfile(fpath):\n            fails.append(f"routing file missing: {fname}")\n            continue\n        with open(fpath, encoding="utf-8") as f:\n            text = f.read()\n        for cand in sorted(set(re.findall(r"`([^`\\s]+/[^`\\s]+)`", text))):\n            if cand.startswith("http") or any(c in cand for c in "<>*{}|"):\n                continue\n            p = cand.rstrip("/")\n            if p.startswith("~"):\n                p = os.path.expanduser(p)\n            ap = p if p.startswith("/") else os.path.join(BRAIN, p)\n            if not os.path.exists(ap):\n                fails.append(f"dead path in {fname}: `{cand}`")\n\n    # (g) undeclared claude/codex asymmetry, measured against skills/PARITY.md.\n    parity = os.path.join(DB, "PARITY.md")\n    if not os.path.isfile(parity):\n        fails.append("PARITY.md missing (skill asymmetry baseline)")\n    else:\n        with open(parity, encoding="utf-8") as f:\n            ptext = f.read()\n        # A bare "- name" list cannot carry the reason each split needs, and a\n        # baseline without reasons is a snapshot rather than a decision (that is\n        # exactly how the 2026-07-19 baseline went unaudited). So names are read\n        # from `backticks` anywhere in the file, which lets each entry sit beside\n        # its justification in prose. Only tokens that name a real skill count —\n        # that ignores the incidental backticks (paths, flags, MCP servers)\n        # without needing them escaped, and a typo\'d name simply fails to\n        # declare, surfacing as undeclared drift rather than passing silently.\n        known = claude_db | codex_db\n        declared = {t for t in re.findall(r"`([A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*)`", ptext)\n                    if t in known}\n        for n in sorted((claude_db - codex_db) - declared):\n            fails.append(f"undeclared parity drift: skills/claude/{n} has no codex counterpart "\n                         f"(activate for both, or declare in skills/PARITY.md)")\n        for n in sorted((codex_db - claude_db) - declared):\n            fails.append(f"undeclared parity drift: skills/codex/{n} has no claude counterpart "\n                         f"(activate for both, or declare in skills/PARITY.md)")\n\n        # (h) effective runtime parity. Project activations can satisfy a skill\n        # absent from the home-level root and vice versa. External plugin skills\n        # do not have to live in this DB, but unexplained one-runtime availability\n        # is still drift worth surfacing.\n        effective = {}\n        for runtime, roots in RUNTIME_ACTIVE_ROOTS.items():\n            names = set()\n            for root in roots:\n                if os.path.isdir(root):\n                    names.update(\n                        n for n in os.listdir(root)\n                        if not n.startswith(".")\n                        and os.path.isfile(os.path.join(root, n, "SKILL.md"))\n                    )\n            effective[runtime] = names\n        for n in sorted((effective["claude"] - effective["codex"]) - declared):\n            fails.append(f"undeclared activation drift: {n} is available to Claude but not Codex")\n        for n in sorted((effective["codex"] - effective["claude"]) - declared):\n            fails.append(f"undeclared activation drift: {n} is available to Codex but not Claude")\n\n    # (i) instruction twins. Frozen, imported, and vendor reference material is\n    # not an active project scope and is intentionally excluded.\n    ignored_parts = {".git", "archive", "graph-imports", "node_modules", "raw", "reference", "_snapshots", "anthropic-capture"}\n    for current, dirs, files in os.walk(BRAIN):\n        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in ignored_parts]\n        present = set(files)\n        if "CLAUDE.md" in present and "AGENTS.md" not in present:\n            fails.append(f"missing AGENTS.md peer: {os.path.relpath(current, BRAIN)}")\n        if "AGENTS.md" in present and "CLAUDE.md" not in present:\n            fails.append(f"missing CLAUDE.md peer: {os.path.relpath(current, BRAIN)}")\n\n    # (j) project-scoped Codex agents.\n    agent_catalog = os.path.join(BRAIN, "agents", "codex")\n    agent_active = os.path.join(BRAIN, ".codex", "agents")\n    if os.path.isdir(agent_catalog):\n        for name in sorted(n for n in os.listdir(agent_catalog) if n.endswith(".toml")):\n            source = os.path.join(agent_catalog, name)\n            try:\n                with open(source, "rb") as handle:\n                    data = tomllib.load(handle)\n            except (OSError, tomllib.TOMLDecodeError) as exc:\n                fails.append(f"invalid Codex agent TOML: agents/codex/{name}: {exc}")\n                continue\n            for field in ("name", "description", "developer_instructions"):\n                if not data.get(field):\n                    fails.append(f"Codex agent missing {field}: agents/codex/{name}")\n            activated = os.path.join(agent_active, name)\n            if not os.path.isfile(activated):\n                fails.append(f"Codex agent not activated: .codex/agents/{name}")\n            elif not filecmp.cmp(source, activated, shallow=False):\n                fails.append(f"stale Codex agent activation: .codex/agents/{name}")\n\n    if fails:\n        for msg in fails:\n            print(f"skills: FAIL — {msg}", file=sys.stderr)\n        print(f"skills: FAIL ({len(fails)} problems)", file=sys.stderr)\n        sys.exit(1)\n    print(f"skills: PASS (claude={len(claude_db)} codex={len(codex_db)} DB skills; "\n          f"activations resolve; INDEX complete)")\n\nif __name__ == "__main__":\n    main()\n\r\n'}]

The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n# domain.md\n\nCopy path\n\n\n# Domain Docs\n\nHow the engineering skills should consume this repo\'s domain documentation when exploring the codebase.\n\n## Before exploring, read these\n\n- **`CONTEXT.md`** at the repo root, or\n\n- **`CONTEXT-MAP.md`** at the repo root if it exists: it points at one `CONTEXT.md` per context. Read each one relevant to the topic.\n\n- **`docs/adr/`**: read ADRs that touch the area you\'re about to work in. In multi-context repos, also check `src/<context>/docs/adr/` for context-scoped decisions.\n\nIf any of these files don\'t exist, **proceed silently**. Don\'t flag their absence; don\'t suggest creating them upfront. The `/domain-modeling` skill (reached via `/grill-with-docs` and `/improve-codebase-architecture`) creates them lazily when terms or decisions actually get resolved.\n\n## File structure\n\nSingle-context repo (most repos):\n\n```\n\n/\n\n├── CONTEXT.md\n\n├── docs/adr/\n\n│ ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\n\n│ └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\n\n└── src/\n\n```\n\nMulti-context repo (presence of `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the root):\n\n```\n\n/\n\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\n\n├── docs/adr/ ← system-wide decisions\n\n└── src/\n\n├── ordering/\n\n│ ├── CONTEXT.md\n\n│ └── docs/adr/ ← context-specific decisions\n\n└── billing/\n\n├── CONTEXT.md\n\n└── docs/adr/\n\n```\n\n## Use the glossary\'s vocabulary\n\nWhen your output names a domain concept (in an issue title, a refactor proposal, a hypothesis, a test name), use the term as defined in `CONTEXT.md`. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\nL349: \nL350: ---\nL351: # Domain Modeling\nL352: \nL353: Actively build and sharpen the project\'s domain model as you design. This is the *active* discipline: challenging terms, inventing edge-case scenarios, and writing the glossary and decisions down the moment they crystallise. (Merely *reading* `CONTEXT.md` for vocabulary is not this skill: that\'s a one-line habit any skill can do. This skill is for when you\'re changing the model, not just consuming it.)\nL354: ## File structure\nL355: \nL356: Most repos have a single context:\nL357: \nL358: ```\nL359: \nL360: /\nL361: \nL362: ├── CONTEXT.md\nL363: \nL364: ├── docs/\nL365: \nL366: │ └── adr/\nL367: \nL368: │ ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\nL369: \nL370: │ └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\nL371: \nL372: └── src/\nL373: \nL374: ```\nL375: \nL376: If a `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists at the root, the repo has multiple contexts. The map points to where each one lives:\nL377: \nL378: ```\nL379: \nL380: /\nL381: \nL382: ├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\nL383: \nL384: ├── docs/\nL385: \nL386: │ └── adr/ ← system-wide decisions\nL387: \nL388: ├── src/\nL389: \nL390: │ ├── ordering/\nL391: \nL392: │ │ ├── CONTEXT.md\nL393: \nL394: │ │ └── docs/adr/ ← context-specific decisions\nL395: \nL396: │ └── billing/\nL397: \nL398: │ ├── CONTEXT.md\nL399: │ └── docs/adr/\nL400: \nL401: ```\nL402: \nL403: Create files lazily: only when you have something to write. If no `CONTEXT.md` exists, create one when the first term is resolved. If no `docs/adr/` exists, create it when the first ADR is needed.\nL404: ## During the session\nL405: \nL406: ### Challenge against the glossary\nL407: \nL408: When the user uses a term that conflicts with the existing language in `CONTEXT.md`, call it out immediately. "Your glossary defines \'cancellation\' as X, but you seem to mean Y. Which is it?"\nL409: \nL410: ### Sharpen fuzzy language\nL411: \nL412: When the user uses vague or overloaded terms, propose a precise canonical term. "You\'re saying \'account\': do you mean the Customer or the User? Those are different things."\nL413: ### Discuss concrete scenarios\nL414: \nL415: When domain relationships are being discussed, stress-test them with specific scenarios. Invent scenarios that probe edge cases and force the user to be precise about the boundaries between concepts.\nL416: \nL417: ### Cross-reference with code\nL418: \nL419: When the user states how something works, check whether the code agrees. If you find a contradiction, surface it: "Your code cancels entire Orders, but you just said partial cancellation is possible. Which is right?"\nL420: ### Update CONTEXT.md inline\nL421: \nL422: When a term is resolved, update `CONTEXT.md` right there. Don\'t batch these up: capture them as they happen. Use the format in [CONTEXT-FORMAT.md](./CONTEXT-FORMAT.md).\nL423: \nL424: `CONTEXT.md` should be totally devoid of implementation details. Do not treat `CONTEXT.md` as a spec, a scratch pad, or a repository for implementation decisions. It is a glossary and nothing else.\nL425: ### Offer ADRs sparingly\nL426: \nL427: Only offer to create an ADR when all three are true:\nL428: \nL429: 1. **Hard to reverse**: the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful\nL430: \nL431: 2. **Surprising without context**: a future reader will wonder "why did they do it this way?"\nL432: \nL433: 3. **The result of a real trade-off**: there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons\nL434: \nL435: If any of the three is missing, skip the ADR. Use the format in [ADR-FORMAT.md](./ADR-FORMAT.md).\nL436: \nL437: You can’t perform that action at this time.\n'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'grilling: rounds asked dependent questions together, fix with frontier gate + pivot ordering · Issue #895 · mattpocock/skills · GitHub (https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/issues/895)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn3search0\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 weeks ago; Crawled: 3 weeks ago;     `Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding.\n\n# grilling: rounds asked dependent questions together, fix with frontier gate + pivot ordering\xa0#895\n\nNew issue\n\nCopy link\n\nNew issue\n\nCopy link\n\nOpen\n\nOpen\n\ngrilling: rounds asked dependent questions together, fix with frontier gate + pivot ordering#895\n\nCopy link\n\n## Description\n\nsaurondark22\n\nopened on Aug 18, 2026\n\nIssue body actions\n\n## The problem\n\nI kept hitting a case where dependent questions got asked in the same batch. Worst was five at once, and answering Q1 made the next four moot or changed their scope. "The whole frontier" was being read loosely enough that decisions whose fate still hung on an open answer slipped in.\nOn top of that, the agent kept asking obvious things such as variable names, trivial choices that I want agent to infer. Grilling should only ask about decisions where a wrong guess forces rework or reshapes the tree, not surface-level stuff the model can infer fine.\n\n## Proposed fix\n\nAdd frontier as an explicit gate and pivots as the ordering within a round. The old "do not act until the user confirms" read as implication too often.\n  * Rebuilt the question template with stacked (A)/(B)/(C) options on their own lines. The old single-line option block kept collapsing into one paragraph.\n  * Added "for trivial decisions, assume your recommended answer" to cut the variable-name-tier noise. It\'s a model judgment call, which I\'m not fully comfortable with, but the noise reduction is worth it. Open to a better wording.\n\n## Skill Content\n    \n    `Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\n    \n    Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven\'t heard yet. Ask pivots first within the frontier, details in later rounds. A pivot is a decision whose answer could reshape the tree. Within each round, batch only questions that would still need asking no matter how the open answers resolve: if one question\'s answer could change whether another belongs at all, defer it to a later round. Then wait for the user\'s answers before the next round.\n    \n    Number questions in incremental order, never reuse or skip a questions number, until user ask to restart grilling session. Each question should have easy readable and formatted as below template:\n    \\```\n    ❓ Q<N> - "<question topic>": <question body, might be multiple separate paragraphs.>\n    \n    (A) <option 1>\n    (B) <option 2>\n    ...\n    \n    ➡️ <then your recommended option(s) and why>\n    \\```\n    \n    Never ask for anything you could look up yourself. For trivial decisions, assume your recommended answer. Run fact-finding in parallel. Never block the round on it.\n    \n    The session ends when no unresolved decisions remain. Never act until the user explicitly confirms shared understanding. Acting includes planning, summarizing, implementing. Confirmation is not silence, partial answers, or answering a question. When in doubt, ask.\n    `\n\nRemove `\\`, it\'s just to prevent the code block from breaking here--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nUse host\'s question tool for grilling sessions · Issue #840 · mattpocock/skills · GitHub (https://github.com/mattpocock/skills/issues/840)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn3search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: last month; Crawled: last month;     Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. ...     A free-text answer replaces option selection for that question; the user does not need to provide both.\n\n# Use host\'s question tool for grilling sessions\xa0#840\n\nNew issue\n\nCopy link\n\nNew issue\n\nCopy link\n\nOpen\n\nOpen\n\nUse host\'s question tool for grilling sessions#840\n\n## Description\n\nNeonLaserEffect\n\nopened on Aug 12, 2026\n\nIssue body actions\n\nIn general I like that a grilling session asks questions in batches, but I don\'t like the wall of text and how to answer everything in batches in plain text. I would prefer to get the questions presented with the host\'s question tool, so questions are less overwhelming and more comfortable to answer.\nIn a local project I have used the writing-for-agents-skill to adjust that. Maybe this is something that other people might like better as well:\n    \n    `---\n    name: grilling\n    description:\n      Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or\n      uses any \'grill\' trigger phrases.\n    ---\n    \n    Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision\nWhen a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem,\n    tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it — don\'t ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don\'t block\n    on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent\n    to report — ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user\'s — put each to them and wait.\n    \n    The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed.\n    Ask for explicit confirmation with a dedicated yes/no question: "Does this design tree reflect our shared\n    understanding?" Reopen the affected branch after a negative answer.
